- **CLI** (`codeqlick` 0.5.0; the project linked in the folder, or `--project`): `codeqlick hooks` lists them; `codeqlick hooks add <name> [--branch <branch>]` makes one (the production branch when `--branch` is missing) and prints its URL once, with a note to paste it in the CMS's webhook; `codeqlick hooks rm <id or name>` deletes one. `--json` prints the API's answer (for `add`, with the `url`).

#### Web analytics (phase 2)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13). A project counts its visits without cookies and without sending its visitors to a third party, and sees them in the console. Decision 44.

- **Switch:** off by default; a project's admins switch it on in its **Analytics** tab (`CloudProject.analytics`). While it is off, events for the project are dropped.
- **Snippet:** `<script defer src="https://api.codeqlick.com/v1/cloud/analytics/script.js"></script>` in the site's pages (sent with `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: cross-origin`, cached a day). The script (under 1 KB, no cookies, no local storage) sends a page view on load and on every navigation of a single-page app (`history.pushState`, `replaceState`, `popstate`) with `navigator.sendBeacon` as `text/plain` JSON `{ n, u, r, w }` (event name, the page's address, the referrer, the window's width). `window.codeqlick.track('<name>')` sends a custom event (letters, digits, `_`, `-`, `.`, `:`; up to 40). It sends nothing over `http:`, from `localhost` or when the browser asks "Do Not Track". A site with a Content-Security-Policy allows `https://api.codeqlick.com` in `script-src` and `connect-src`.
- **Endpoint:** `POST /v1/cloud/analytics/event` (public, 4 KB, 60 a minute per IP), always `202` so nobody learns which addresses count. An event is kept only when its page `u` is one of the project's addresses (`https://<slug>.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>`, its deployments' and its verified domains; like forms), the `Origin` header (when sent) is that page's origin, the project has analytics on and is neither suspended nor deleted, and the User-Agent isn't a bot (empty, or naming a crawler, a headless browser, a link preview or an HTTP library).
- **What is kept** (`CloudAnalyticsEvent`): the project, the event name (`pageview` or the custom one), the path (no query, no fragment; up to 300), the referrer's host when it is another site, the campaign (`utm_source`, or `ref`, up to 60), the browser, the operating system and the device (mobile under 768 px, tablet under 1,024, desktop), and a **visitor id**: the first 16 bytes of SHA-256 of a daily salt, the project, the IP and the User-Agent. The salt is random, one per UTC day (`CloudAnalyticsSalt`), and deleted the day after, so ids can't be recomputed or followed from one day to the next: a person is counted once a day. No IP, no cookie, no country.
- **Limits:** 50,000 events a month per team on the free plan (`analyticsEventsPerMonth`, Madrid calendar month; over it events are dropped and the owners get one email that month). Events are kept 365 days, then deleted (daily).
- **Report:** `GET /v1/cloud/projects/:id/analytics?period=24h|7d|30d|90d|365d` (default `7d`) answers the totals (visitors, page views), a series (by hour for `24h`, by Madrid day otherwise), and the top 10 pages, referrers (with campaigns), browsers, operating systems, devices and custom events, each with visitors (and page views or counts).
- **Console:** the project's **Analytics** tab: the switch, the snippet to copy, the period, the totals, a bar chart and the tables.
- **Privacy:** the team decides to measure and is the controller; Codeqlick processes the events for it (Cloud terms). Nothing is stored on visitors' devices and no personal data is kept, which is how it avoids needing a cookie banner.

#### Key-value store (phase 2)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13). Each project has a small key-value store reached over HTTP, for settings, feature flags, counters and data that scripts, CI jobs and servers share; keys under `public:` are readable by anyone, so a static site can read them from the browser. Decision 45.

- **Entry** (`CloudKvEntry`, by project and key): a key (1–256 characters: letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, `:`), a value (any JSON, up to 64 KB once serialised), its size, an optional expiry (`ttl` 1 second to 365 days) and when it was created and updated. An expired entry reads as missing and is deleted within the hour.
- **Limits:** free plan 1,000 keys and 10 MB per project (`kvKeysPerProject`, `kvBytesPerProject`; past them a write answers `409`); 600 requests a minute per caller.
- **API** (`/v1/cloud/projects/:id/kv`, session or API token; any member reads and writes): list keys (`?prefix&cursor&limit`, by key), read one, write one (`PUT { value, ttl? }`), delete one, and increment one atomically (`POST …/incr { by?, ttl? }`, `by` an integer, default 1; a missing key starts at 0; `409` when the value isn't an integer). Writes aren't recorded as team events (they are data, not changes to the team).
- **Public reads:** `GET /v1/cloud/kv/<project slug>/<key>` answers the value of a key starting with `public:` as JSON to anyone (CORS for any origin, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=10`, 120 a minute per IP); any other key, a suspended or a deleted project answers `404`. Nothing is writable without a token.
- **Console:** the project's **KV** tab: usage against the limits, keys by prefix with their size and expiry, a key's value, writing one (JSON) and deleting it, and the `curl` lines to use it.

#### Plans and billing (phase 2)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13). A team moves to **PRO** (the limits of `CLOUD_PLANS.PRO`) by paying a monthly subscription through Stripe; the free plan stays. Decision 46.

- **Price:** a recurring monthly price made in Stripe's dashboard, named by `STRIPE_CLOUD_PRO_PRICE_ID`; its amount and currency are read from Stripe (cached an hour) and shown in the console, plus IVA (the same tax rate as decision 20, `SITE_VAT_PERCENT`). Without Stripe's keys or that price the team's **Plan** page says paid plans aren't available yet and nothing else changes.
- **Upgrade:** a team's owners press "Upgrade to PRO": `POST /v1/cloud/teams/:team/billing/checkout` makes a Checkout Session in subscription mode (the team's Stripe customer, made once with the owner's email and the team's id in its metadata; `client_reference_id` and metadata name the team) and the console sends them there. Stripe's webhook (`POST /v1/payments/stripe`, the same endpoint as decision 20, which tells them apart by the session's mode and metadata) makes the team `PRO` when the session completes.
- **Subscription state** (`CloudTeam`: `stripeCustomerId`, `stripeSubscriptionId`, `subscriptionStatus`, `currentPeriodEnd`, `cancelAtPeriodEnd`), kept by the webhook's `customer.subscription.created|updated|deleted`: `active` and `trialing` are PRO; `past_due` stays PRO while Stripe retries (the owners are emailed on `invoice.payment_failed`); `canceled`, `unpaid` and `incomplete_expired` go back to FREE. The event's subscription is read again from Stripe before acting, so events arriving out of order don't matter.
- **Managing it:** `POST /v1/cloud/teams/:team/billing/portal` opens Stripe's customer portal (card, invoices, cancel at the end of the period). Owners only; admins and members see the plan.
- **Going back to FREE** deletes nothing: what is over the free limits keeps working but nothing more can be added (a 409 names the limit), and the monthly limits (builds, analytics, form submissions) apply at once.
- **Console:** the team's **Plan** page: the plan, its limits side by side, the price, the subscription's state and next renewal, and the buttons. Admin → Cloud shows each team's plan.

#### Usage alerts (phase 2)

Owner's request (2026-11-13). A team hears that it is close to a plan limit before anything is refused. Decision 54.

- **What is measured**, per team against its current plan (`CLOUD_PLANS`, so PRO teams get alerts against PRO's limits):

  | Key | Used | Limit | Resets |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `builds` | builds queued this Madrid calendar month | `buildsPerMonth` | the 1st |
  | `formSubmissions` | form submissions this month, all the team's forms | `formSubmissionsPerMonth` | the 1st |
  | `analyticsEvents` | analytics events this month | `analyticsEventsPerMonth` | the 1st |
  | `storage` | bytes of the team's kept deployments | `storageBytesPerTeam` | never (frees up when deployments are deleted) |
  | `deploymentsPerHour` | deployments in the last hour (rolling) | `deploymentsPerHour` | rolling |

  The counts are the ones the limits themselves use, so an alert and a refusal never disagree. A limit counts as reached at `used × 100 ≥ limit × CLOUD_USAGE_ALERT_PERCENT`, a constant (80), not an environment variable. The percent shown is `floor(used / limit × 100)`, and can pass 100.
- **Sweep:** `CloudUsageAlertsService` (API process, every 10 minutes, off in tests, `sweep(now)` callable directly) looks for the teams with activity since the last sweep: a build queued, a form submission, an analytics event or a deployment created (by their timestamps; teams with none are not looked at). The first sweep after a start looks back an hour, so a restart loses nothing. For each such team it computes the five measures once and, for each one at or over the threshold, tries to claim an alert (below). Nothing runs on the hot paths (posting a form, recording an event, deploying), which stay as cheap as they are.
- **Once per limit per month:** `CloudUsageAlert` has one row per team, key and period. The period is the Madrid month (`yyyy-mm`) for every key, including storage and deployments per hour, so each limit mails at most once a month. The claim is an `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on the unique index; only the sweep that inserted the row sends, so two sweeps (or two API processes) never send twice. As with the existing limit emails, a failed send is logged and not retried that month. Rows older than 13 months are deleted by the sweep once a day.
- **Email:** to every `OWNER` of the team (admins and members don't get it: owners are the ones who pay and choose the plan), each in their own language, in the branded layout (`email-layout.ts`; `cloudUsageAlertEmail` in `cloud-emails.ts`; plain text and HTML). Subject, for example: "Your team *web* used 80 % of its builds this month" (Spanish: "Tu equipo *web* ha usado el 80 % de sus builds este mes"); the percent is the real one at that moment. The body gives used and limit ("164 of 200"), when it resets ("on the 1st, Madrid time" for the monthly ones; storage: "when you delete deployments"; deployments per hour: "as the last hour passes"), what happens at 100 % (below), and a button to the team's usage page (`cloud.codeqlick.com/{locale}/console/<team>`). For a `FREE` team, and only when billing is available (`CloudBillingService.available`), a line says PRO has higher limits, with a link to the Plan page. The existing emails at 100 % (form submissions, analytics events) stay as they are and usually come after this one.
- **At 100 %** (the existing behaviour, repeated in the email): builds are refused, with a note in the console and a failed commit status; form posts get `429`; analytics events are dropped; deployments are refused (`413`, storage; `429`, per hour).
- **Console banner:** every team page shows a warning banner above its content while any limit is at 80 % or more, and an error-styled one while any is at 100 % or more, naming the limits and linking to the usage page (the team's main page). It is always shown while true; nothing is dismissed or stored. It is computed from the usage endpoint (below), which gives each measure's `percent`; the frontend only decides the style.
- **Team events:** an alert sent is recorded as `usage.alert_sent`, a system event (`actorId` null, `actorName` "Codeqlick", no token, no IP) with `details` `{ key, percent, used, limit, period, emailed }` (`emailed`: how many owners). It is listed in the events page like the others.
- **No new settings:** the threshold is a constant, the mail is the platform's, and nothing is configured per team. Alerts per person, other thresholds and Slack or webhook delivery are left for later.

#### Databases (phase 2)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13): managed PostgreSQL databases for projects, **for now on the production VPS** in a PostgreSQL of their own, apart from the platform's. Decision 47.

- **Server:** the `cloud-pg` service (`postgres:18-alpine`, its own volume) listening publicly on `pg.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>:5432` with **TLS only** (the hosting domain's wildcard certificate, copied in when the container starts and every 12 hours, then reloaded) and SCRAM passwords. `pg_hba.conf`: remote clients only over `hostssl`, each role only to its own database (`sameuser`); the administrator role only from the Docker network. `pg` is a reserved project slug. Settings in `CLOUD_PG_ADMIN_URL` (the administrator's connection inside Docker) and `CLOUD_PG_HOST`; without them the **Databases** tab says it isn't set up.
- **Database** (`CloudDatabase` of a project; free: 1 per project, PRO: 5): a `name` (shown), and in PostgreSQL a database and a role both called `cq_<16 hex>`, the role `LOGIN NOSUPERUSER NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE`, owner of its database, with a connection limit (free 5, PRO 20) and `statement_timeout` 60 s; `CONNECT` is revoked from `PUBLIC`. The password (32 random characters) is shown once, at creation and when it is reset; it is never stored (decision 42's reasoning: the console doesn't need to read it back).
- **Size:** free 256 MB, PRO 10 GB per database (`pg_database_size`, checked every 10 minutes and shown). Over it, the database becomes read-only (`default_transaction_read_only`, its sessions ended) and the team's admins are emailed; under it again it is writable again. A session can turn read-only off for itself, so past 150 % of its size the role's connection limit becomes 0 until it is back under (a team deletes data through the console's help or by email). Databases of deleted projects are dropped at the next check.
- **Backups:** each database is dumped daily (`pg_dump -Fc`) next to the platform's backups and kept 7 days. For now they stay on the same server, which the console says; restoring is asked by email.
- **API** (`/v1/cloud/projects/:id/databases`, ADMIN to create, reset and delete; members read): list (`{ id, name, host, port, database, user, sizeBytes, maxBytes, readOnly, connectionUrl (without the password), createdAt }`), create (`{ name }`, the answer adds `password` and the full `connectionUrl` once), reset the password, delete (drops it at once; the latest daily dump stays for 7 days). Recorded as team events.
- **Console:** the project's **Databases** tab: the list with size and state, the connection string to copy (`postgresql://<user>:<password>@pg.<domain>:5432/<database>?sslmode=require`), create, reset the password, delete.

#### Object storage (phase 2)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13): S3-compatible buckets for projects, **for now on the production VPS**. Decision 48.

- **Server:** [Garage](https://garagehq.deuxfleurs.fr/) (`dxflrs/garage:v2.5.0`, one node, replication 1, its own volumes) in the `cloud-storage` service. Its S3 API answers at `https://s3.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>` (host Nginx with the wildcard certificate, path-style addresses, region `garage`, no upload size limit, buffering off); its admin API stays inside Docker (`CLOUD_STORAGE_ADMIN_URL`, `CLOUD_STORAGE_ADMIN_TOKEN`). `s3` is a reserved project slug. Without the settings the **Storage** tab says it isn't set up.
- **Bucket** (`CloudBucket` of a project; free: 2 per project, PRO: 10): a name of 3–63 lowercase letters, digits and dashes, unique across Cloud, which is also its name in Garage, and a quota (free 1 GB and 100,000 objects per bucket; PRO 50 GB and 1,000,000) set as Garage's bucket quota, so uploads past it are refused by Garage itself. Size and objects are read from Garage and shown.
- **Access keys** (`CloudBucketKey`, up to 10 per bucket): a name and read-only or read-write; made in Garage for that bucket only. The secret is shown once; only the key's id is kept. Deleting a key deletes it in Garage.
- **Use:** any S3 client or SDK (`endpoint https://s3.<domain>`, `forcePathStyle`, region `garage`); browsers get files through presigned URLs that the team's own server signs. Public buckets and CORS rules are left for later.
- **Deleting a bucket** is refused while it holds objects (empty it first); then it and its keys are deleted.
- **API** (`/v1/cloud/projects/:id/buckets`, ADMIN to change; members read): list (`{ id, name, endpoint, region, sizeBytes, objects, maxBytes, maxObjects, keys: [{ id, name, accessKeyId, write, createdAt }], createdAt }`), create `{ name }`, delete; `POST …/:bucketId/keys` `{ name, write }` → the key with `secretAccessKey` once; `DELETE …/:bucketId/keys/:keyId`.
- **Console:** the project's **Storage** tab: buckets with usage, keys, and the settings to paste into an S3 client.

**Privacy and responsibility of databases and storage:** what teams keep there is theirs (Codeqlick is its processor, Cloud terms); the abuse rules of static hosting apply. Both run on the platform's server until they move to their own (owner's choice, November 2026).

#### Disk guard (one server)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13): everything stays on one machine for now (the platform, builds, `cloud-pg`, Garage and the backups share the VPS's disk), so Cloud must never fill it and take the platform down with it. Decision 49.

- **Measure:** `CloudDiskService` reads the free space of the disk holding `CLOUD_ROOT` (`statfs`) every minute. States: `ok`; `low` when less than `CLOUD_DISK_LOW_GB` is free (default 30); `full` when less than `CLOUD_DISK_MIN_GB` (default 15).
- **While `full`:** creating deployments (CLI uploads and build outputs), databases and buckets answers `507` ("Codeqlick Cloud is short of space; try again later"); every Cloud database is made read-only, as if it were over its size; and every bucket's Garage quota is lowered to its current size, so nothing in Cloud grows. Coming back to `ok` or `low` undoes it at the next check (databases every 10 minutes, quotas within the hour).
- **Builds:** a build is never refused for lack of space, but while the state is `full` the claim sends `cache: null` (build runs without the dependency cache, section "Builds from Git"); its output deployment is refused as above.
- **Telling people:** the server monitor (8.x, `deploy/monitor/monitor.sh`) already emails the team at 85 % of the disk and now also checks `cloud-pg`, `cloud-pg-backup` and `cloud-storage` when the `cloud-data` profile is on. Admin → Cloud shows the disk (size, free, state) and what Cloud keeps on it: deployments, databases and buckets.
- `GET /v1/admin/cloud/disk` (ADMIN): `{ state, totalBytes, freeBytes, lowBytes, minBytes, deploymentsBytes, databasesBytes, bucketsBytes }`.

#### Command line for analytics and the key-value store

`codeqlick` 0.4 (owner's choice 2026-11-13; the project is the one linked in the folder, or `--project`): `codeqlick analytics [--period 24h|7d|30d|90d|365d] [--json]` prints the totals and the top pages and sources; `codeqlick kv ls [prefix]`, `codeqlick kv get <key>`, `codeqlick kv set <key> <value> [--ttl seconds]` (the value is JSON, or text when it isn't; `-` reads standard input), `codeqlick kv rm <key>`, `codeqlick kv incr <key> [--by n]`. `--json` prints the API's answer.

#### Public documentation (docs.codeqlick.com)

Owner's request (2026-11-13), roadmap item "Cloud: public documentation" (CQP-152). Cloud gets a public, indexable documentation site in English and Spanish, for developers who haven't signed up. It replaces the short `/docs` page of the console (section 6.3). Decision 53.

- **Site:** `codeqlick-docs/`, an Astro 7.4.5 static site with TypeScript 6.0.3 (`astro check` refuses 7.0, decision 19). It uses **exactly the dependencies and versions of `codeqlick-sites/web-basica`** (its `package-lock.json` is copied, with the package name changed), so there is no new package to audit; `sharp`, `@astrojs/sitemap` and the formatter come along, and the template's other font packages simply go unused. No MDX, no UI kit, no client framework. Dependabot gets a group for the folder (section 9).
- **Content:** Astro content collections of plain Markdown: `src/content/docs/en/<slug>.md` and `src/content/docs/es/<slug>.md`, the same slugs in both languages. Frontmatter: `title`, `description` (used for the page's `<meta>` and in search), `group` (the sidebar group: `start`, `hosting`, `services` or `reference`), `order` and optional `draft: true`. A draft page is left out of the build: no route, no sidebar entry, no search entry, no sitemap line. Pages for features whose code is not merged yet are written with `draft: true`; whoever merges the feature removes the flag in the same pull request or right after. A page never links to a draft page except from another draft page (the link check would fail). A page and its twin must have the same `draft` value.
- **Writing:** second person, short sentences, a runnable example for every feature (`codeqlick` commands and `curl`), written for developers from the specs in this section: no server paths, table or service names, or other internals. Features are written as features, not as plans.
- **Routes:** `/en/<slug>/` and `/es/<slug>/`, `/en/` and `/es/` for the introduction. `/` is a tiny page that reads `navigator.language` and sends the visitor to `/en/` or `/es/` with a script; without JavaScript (and for crawlers) it shows two links. Every page has `<link rel="alternate" hreflang="en|es">` to its twin and `hreflang="x-default"` to `/`.
- **Pages** (the same list in both languages, `group` in brackets):

  | Slug | Page | Draft until |
  |---|---|---|
  | `index` | Introduction (what Cloud is, what is free) | |
  | `quickstart` | Quickstart: sign in with the CLI and deploy a folder | |
  | `static-hosting` | Static hosting: deployments, previews, rollbacks, single-page apps, limits | |
  | `custom-domains` | Custom domains: verify, point, states | |
  | `password-protection` | Password protection | its PR is merged (#209) |
  | `codeqlick-json` | `codeqlick.json`: redirects, rewrites, headers | its PR is merged (#211) |
  | `builds-from-git` | Builds from Git: connecting GitHub, build settings, previews of pull requests | |
  | `environment-variables` | Environment variables | |
  | `deploy-hooks` | Deploy hooks | its PR is merged (#210) |
  | `build-notifications` | Build notifications: email, Slack, Discord, signed webhooks | |
  | `forms` | Forms | |
  | `monitors-and-status-pages` | Monitors, heartbeats and status pages | |
  | `website-check` | Website check API and the GitHub Actions use | |
  | `analytics` | Web analytics | |
  | `key-value-store` | Key-value store | |
  | `databases` | Databases | |
  | `object-storage` | Object storage | |
  | `cli` | CLI reference: every command and flag | |
  | `api-tokens` | API tokens and CI (`CODEQLICK_TOKEN`) | |
  | `plans-and-limits` | Plans and limits of the free plan, and PRO | |

  The sidebar groups them as *Get started*, *Hosting* (static hosting to deploy hooks, `build-notifications` included), *Services* (forms to object storage) and *Reference* (CLI, API tokens, plans). Usage alerts are written into `plans-and-limits` when they are merged.
- **Brand:** the tokens of section 7 as CSS custom properties, light and dark through `prefers-color-scheme` (no toggle), the "Click Q" logo inline as SVG next to the lowercase wordmark `codeqlick`, and `docs` as a label. The brand fonts are the **same three variable `woff2` files as the website** (Latin subset, OFL; section 3), copied into `public/fonts/` and loaded with `@font-face` and `font-display: swap`, so they come from the docs' own domain; there is no font package and no request to Google. The stacks fall back to `system-ui` and `ui-monospace`. Code blocks use Astro 7's default Markdown highlighting, styled with the brand tokens (`--code-bg`).
- **Search:** an Astro endpoint `src/pages/search-[lang].json.ts` writes, at build time, `/search-en.json` and `/search-es.json`: for each published page its `title`, `description`, URL, headings (text and anchor) and plain text (up to 20,000 characters; the script cuts a short snippet around the match when it shows a result). A vanilla script (no dependency, under 4 KB gzipped) loads the language's index the first time the box is used, splits the query into lowercase terms, and ranks pages by simple term matching (title weighs most, then headings, then text; every term must match; a heading hit links to its anchor). Keyboard: `/` focuses the box (unless typing in a field), Arrow keys move through the results, Enter opens one, Escape closes the list. Results are in a `role="listbox"` with `aria-activedescendant`. Without JavaScript the box is hidden and the sidebar still lists every page.
- **SEO:** a `<title>` of `<page> · Codeqlick Cloud docs`, a description and a canonical per page, `hreflang` as above, Open Graph (title, description, `og:locale`, one shared image), `@astrojs/sitemap` with its `i18n` option (`en`, `es`), `public/robots.txt` allowing everything and naming `https://docs.codeqlick.com/sitemap-index.xml`, heading anchors, and a 404 page (without canonical or `og:url`, since it answers at any missing path). The site never sets `noindex`: deployment addresses (`--`) get it from Cloud (Static hosting) and `docs.codeqlick.com` doesn't.
- **Checks:** `npm run linkcheck` (`scripts/linkcheck.mjs`, Node only) reads the built `dist/` and fails when an internal `href` or `src` doesn't resolve to a built file or folder with `index.html`, an anchor `#id` doesn't exist in its page, a page lacks its twin in the other language (or the twin's `draft` differs), or a page lacks `<title>`, description or canonical (the 404 page needs none).
- **Hosting (dogfooding):** a Cloud project of the Codeqlick team, slug `codeqlick-docs` (`docs` itself is a reserved slug), connected to this repository with Builds from Git: root folder `codeqlick-docs`, install `npm ci`, build `npm run build`, output `dist`, Node 24, production branch `main`, "build other branches" off and "build pull requests" off (CI builds the site on every pull request, and a Cloud preview for each would only cost builds). Merging to `main` publishes; the Deploy workflow of the platform doesn't touch it. Its custom domain is `docs.codeqlick.com`, allowed by `CLOUD_PLATFORM_DOMAINS` / `CLOUD_PLATFORM_TEAM_ID` (Custom domains). The site makes no API calls, so there is nothing else to configure. Owner's steps once the PR is merged and deployed: create the project and connect it, set the two variables (the team id, not its slug) in `/srv/codeqlick/.env` and redeploy, add the `A` record `docs.codeqlick.com` → `SITES_SERVER_IP` through OVH's DNS, add the domain to the project and verify it with the TXT record, then set `CLOUD_DOCS_URL` (below). Steps in `deploy/README.md`.
- **Console:** with `CLOUD_DOCS_URL` set (frontend server env, e.g. `https://docs.codeqlick.com`; empty by default), `/cloud/docs` answers `307` (temporary) to `<CLOUD_DOCS_URL>/<locale>/`, and every link to the documentation in the console, the landing and the emails uses `<CLOUD_DOCS_URL>/<locale>/` too (one helper, `cloudDocsUrl(locale)`). Without it nothing changes: the console keeps its own `/docs` page (`src/content/cloud-docs.ts`), which is removed in a later change once the site is live. The CLI package's `homepage` becomes `https://docs.codeqlick.com` (metadata only: no release, no version bump).
- **Out of scope:** versioned docs, comments, a language switcher that keeps the section's anchor, an API reference generated from OpenAPI (the live `/docs` of non-production environments stays the reference for the API's shapes), an "edit on GitHub" link.

#### Later phases (outline)

- **Later for databases and storage:** their own servers with off-server backups, public buckets and CORS rules.
- **3 Apps:** containers from a Dockerfile, isolated in microVMs, on their own servers.

### 4.14 Newsletter (`modules/newsletter`)

Roadmap "Newsletter sign-up" (CQP-30): blog readers had no way to follow new posts besides RSS. A reader gives their email and language; every new post is emailed to them once.

- **Subscribers** are `NewsletterSubscriber` rows (section 4.2), not accounts: readers need no account (decision 50).
- **Sign-up is double opt-in.** `POST /newsletter` always answers `202`, so it never reveals whether an address is subscribed. A disposable address is refused as in `/auth/register` (`isDisposableEmail`, field error `email: disposable`). A filled honeypot (`website`) answers `202` and does nothing.
  - A new address is created as `PENDING`. A `PENDING` or `UNSUBSCRIBED` one gets a new confirmation token (48 hours) and becomes or stays `PENDING`; an `UNSUBSCRIBED` address is never reactivated without a new confirmation, so nobody can resubscribe someone who left. It receives "Confirm your subscription" with a link to `SITE_URL/{locale}/newsletter/confirm?token=…`, at most one such email per address every 10 minutes (`confirmSentAt`; inside that time nothing is sent). An `ACTIVE` address gets nothing.
  - The confirmation page asks for a click (a button and a Server Action), so a mail scanner that opens the link cannot confirm a sign-up made by someone else. `POST /newsletter/confirm` with the token sets `ACTIVE` and `confirmedAt` and clears the token and its expiry. `400` for an unknown or expired token. The `confirmedAt` date and the stored language are the proof of consent.
- **Unsubscribing** is one step and needs no sign-in. Every newsletter email (not the confirmation) carries `List-Unsubscribe: <PUBLIC_API_URL/newsletter/unsubscribe/:token>` and `List-Unsubscribe-Post: List-Unsubscribe=One-Click` (RFC 8058, which Gmail and Yahoo require of bulk senders), and a visible link to `SITE_URL/{locale}/newsletter/unsubscribe?token=…`. That page shows a button and the Server Action unsubscribes, so a link scanner opening the link with a GET cannot unsubscribe anyone. A mail client without one-click support opens the header's address in a browser: `GET /newsletter/unsubscribe/:token` changes nothing and redirects (302) to that page in the subscriber's language (`en` for an unknown token, so it reveals nothing). Unsubscribing sets `UNSUBSCRIBED` and `unsubscribedAt` and keeps the row, so a later sign-up is told apart; it is idempotent. `MailMessage` (`modules/mail`) gains optional `headers`, passed to Nodemailer, for the two headers.
- **Sending** (`NewsletterService`, section 4.6): every 10 minutes it takes the posts that are live (`liveWhere()`: published and `publishedAt <= now`, so a scheduled post goes out when it appears) with `newsletterSentAt` null, oldest first, at most 3 per run (the rest wait for the next one). For each post it claims it **first** by setting `newsletterSentAt` only where it is still null (one atomic update; a post another process already claimed is skipped), so a post is emailed at most once even with two API processes or if the run crashes (a crash loses that email rather than sending it twice); then, only when `post.newsletter` is true, it emails every `ACTIVE` subscriber (the list is read again for each post), one by one with a 200 ms pause, in the subscriber's language: the post's title and excerpt and a link to `SITE_URL/{locale}/blog/{slug}`. The translation is the subscriber's language, falling back to English (`pickTranslation`; English always exists). Emails are branded like every other (plain text plus HTML, `email-layout.ts`). A failed send is logged and skipped, never retried; each post logs how many were sent and how many failed. A post with `newsletter` false is marked sent without emailing anyone. The migration sets `newsletterSentAt = now()` and `newsletter = false` on every post already live (a post scheduled for later still goes out), so nothing old is emailed and the editor truthfully says it was not.
- **Cleanup:** daily, `PENDING` subscribers whose `confirmExpiresAt` is more than 7 days past are deleted (nobody confirmed; about 9 days after sign-up, as the privacy policy says), and so are `UNSUBSCRIBED` rows whose `unsubscribedAt` is more than 12 months past (the address and date are kept that long only to respect and prove the opt-out).
- **Limits:** `POST /newsletter` 5/min/IP, `POST /newsletter/confirm` 10/min/IP, `POST /newsletter/unsubscribe` 10/min/IP, the one-click `POST /newsletter/unsubscribe/:token` 60/min/IP (mail providers post from few addresses), `GET /newsletter/unsubscribe/:token` 30/min/IP.
- **Admin:** Admin → Newsletter lists subscribers with the counts per status, filtered by status and by part of the email, and deletes one on request (erasure; it can sign up again). The activity log records the deletion by id only, never the address. The post editor has "Email it to subscribers" (`newsletter`), disabled once `newsletterSentAt` is set, which it then shows.
- **Privacy:** the data kept is the email, the language and the consent dates. The privacy policy (`content/legal`) says so: purpose, consent as the basis, unsubscribing from any email, unconfirmed sign-ups deleted after about 9 days, after unsubscribing only the address and date kept for 12 months, deletion on request at once.

## 5. API contract (v1)

Base URL: `https://api.codeqlick.com/v1`. JSON in and out. Dates are ISO 8601 strings. Lists are paginated:

```json
{ "items": [ … ], "page": 1, "pageSize": 20, "total": 57, "totalPages": 3 }
```

Query params for lists: `page` (default 1), `pageSize` (default 20, max 100).

### Public

| Method | Path | Body / query | Response |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/health` | | Terminus health result |
| POST | `/leads` | `{ name, email, company?, projectType?, message, locale, website? }` (`projectType`: web, custom, mobile, design, support or not-sure; `budget?` is still accepted from older pages) (`website` is a honeypot and must be empty) | `201 { id }` — limited to 5/min/IP |
| GET | `/posts` | `?locale=en&page&pageSize&tag&q` | Paginated `PostSummary` (published only). `q` (max 100 characters) keeps posts whose title, excerpt or content contains it in the requested locale or English, ignoring case and accents (Postgres `unaccent`); `%` and `_` are literal |
| GET | `/posts/tags` | `?locale=en` → `[{ slug, name, count }]`: tags used by live posts, named in `locale`, by name |
| GET | `/posts/:slug/related` | `?locale=en&limit=3` (1–6) → `PostSummary[]`: live posts sharing the most tags, newest first among equals, topped up with the latest posts |
| GET | `/posts/:slug` | `?locale=en` | `PostDetail` or 404 |
| GET | `/portfolio` | `?locale=en&page&pageSize&testimonials=true&technology&service` | Paginated `PortfolioSummary` (published only, by `sortOrder`); `testimonials=true` keeps those with a testimonial in `locale`; `technology` (exact name) and `service` (key) filter and combine |
| GET | `/portfolio/filters` | — | `{ technologies: { name, count }[], services: { key, count }[] }` over published case studies, for the list's filter chips |
| GET | `/portfolio/:slug` | `?locale=en` | `PortfolioDetail` or 404 |
| GET | `/brief` | `Authorization: Bearer <release key>` → the key's project's brief (`{ project: { id, name }, status, answers, ... }`) | |
| GET / POST | `/tasks` | `Authorization: Bearer <release key>`. GET `?status=TODO|IN_PROGRESS|DONE|open` → the key's project's `ProjectTask[]` (open first: in progress, to do; then done). POST `{ title, description? }` → `201` task | |
| PATCH | `/tasks/:taskId` | Release key + `{ title?, description?, status? }` → task; `DONE` may resolve its request. Limited to 60/min/IP | |
| GET | `/bookings/slots` | `{ durationMinutes, timeZone: 'Europe/Madrid', slots: string[] }` (ISO instants); `404` until published | |
| GET | `/reviews` | `?locale=en&limit=6` (1–12) → published reviews written in that language (`{ quote, author, role, rating }`), newest first | |
| POST | `/bookings` | `{ name, email, company?, message?, locale, startsAt, website? }` (`website`: honeypot) | `201 { id, startsAt, meetingUrl }`; `409` when the time isn't free (any more). Emails the visitor (their language, Meet link, cancel link, invitation) and the team inbox (invitation, reply-to the visitor) — limited to 5/min/IP |
| POST | `/bookings/cancel` | `{ token }` (from the visitor's email) | `200 { startsAt }`; `404` for an unknown or already cancelled call. Emails the team |
| POST | `/forms/:key` | A client site's contact form: `{ name, email, phone?, message, website? (honeypot), redirect? }`, urlencoded (a plain HTML form) or JSON | Emails the project's `recipients` (Spanish, reply-to the visitor); with a managed site it also keeps the message (`SiteFormMessage`) and the email links to it in the portal. A form post is answered `303` to `redirect` or the `Referer` with `form=ok` / `form=error` added, only when that URL is on the project's production or staging origin (else a short page); JSON gets `{ ok }`. `403` when `Origin` is another site; `404` for an unknown or disabled key — limited to 5/min/IP |
| GET | `/domains/availability?name=` | A label (`peluqueria-ana`, accents and spaces become dashes) or a domain | `{ results: [{ domain, available: true \| false \| null }] }`: a label in `.com`, `.es` and `.eu`; a domain first, then its label in the other two. OVH's order API answers when its keys are set; otherwise RDAP (404 free, 200 taken; `null` when unknown, e.g. `.es`). Cached 10 min, 400 without a usable label, limited to 20/min/IP |
| POST | `/site-requests` | The basic-website request: `{ preset, business: { name, kind, city, address?, phone?, whatsapp?, email?, hours? (a line per slot, "Days: hours"), services? (a line each, "Service — price"), about?, wishes? (what they want on the website, in their words) }, domain?: { name, owned }, name, email, locale, website? (honeypot) }` | `201 { id, previewUrl? }`: a lead (`projectType: web`, `company` = the business, the message a readable summary with the link to the preview's waiting page) keeping `siteRequest` (with the domain they want, `owned: false`, or have); onboarding pre-fills the site's domain with it; the usual lead emails. No photos (decision 13). Limited to 3/min/IP. Onboarding with that lead fills the site from it: business name and tagline, contact, address, hours, services and about, `schemaType: LocalBusiness`; the preset's made-up services, reviews and facts are dropped |
| GET | `/meetings/rooms/by-code/:code` | `{ code, title, ownerName, live }` of a meeting room (section 4.8); `404` when there is none, `400` for something that can't be a code. Limited to 60/min/IP |
| POST | `/meetings/rooms/:code/guest-ticket` | `{ name }` (2–40) → `{ ticket, expiresIn: 30, iceServers, room: { code, title } }`: a guest's single-use ticket into the room. Limited to 10/min/IP |
| GET | `/citas/p/:slug` | A booking page (section 4.9): `{ slug, title, description, ownerName, durationMinutes, location, video, askPhone, timeZone, enabled, slots }` (`slots`: free starts, ISO); `404` when there is none |
| POST | `/citas/p/:slug/appointments` | `{ startsAt, name, email, phone?, note?, locale, website? }` → the appointment `{ id, slug, title, ownerName, startsAt, endsAt, timeZone, location, meetingCode, status }`; `400` without a required phone, `409` when paused or the time isn't free. Limited to 5/min/IP |
| POST | `/citas/cancel/preview` | `{ token }` → that appointment; `404` for an unknown token |
| POST | `/citas/cancel` | `{ token }` → the cancelled appointment (again if already cancelled); `409` once it has started |
| POST | `/swipe/takedown` | `{ email, photoRef?, reason, details, locale, website? }` → `{ id }`: a removal request (section 4.10); a matching `photoRef` hides that photo at once. Limited to 5/min/IP |
| POST | `/errors` | `{ source: server \| browser, message, stack?, method?, path?, digest?, userAgent? }` → 204: the website reports an error (section 4.11). Limited to 30/min/IP |
| GET | `/videochat/online` | Public: `{ online }`, people connected to the video chat right now (cached 10 s). Limited to 60/min/IP |
| GET | `/site-requests/previews/:slug` | Public: whether a request's preview is online → `{ status, url, business, purchase }`; `purchase` is `{ priceCents (before VAT), vatPercent, totalCents, currency, available }` while buying online is on (`available`: READY, not bought, not a client yet), else null. 404 when there is none. Limited to 30/min/IP |
| POST | `/site-requests/previews/:slug/checkout` | Public: `{ locale, accept: true }` (the terms and starting at once) → `{ url }`, a Stripe Checkout Session to pay the website (section 8.2). 404 while buying online is off or without a preview; 409 when it isn't READY, was bought or its lead is a client. Limited to 5/min/IP |
| GET | `/site-requests/purchases/:sessionId` | Public: how a purchase is going, for the page Stripe returns to → `{ status, business, siteUrl? }` (`siteUrl` once its site exists); 404 when unknown |
| POST | `/payments/stripe` | Stripe's webhook (raw body, `Stripe-Signature` checked with `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, else 400): a paid `checkout.session.completed` or `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded` marks the purchase PAID and makes the site; `checkout.session.expired` marks it EXPIRED. Answers 200 to anything else |
| POST | `/site-audits` | The free website check (section 4.12): `{ url, email, name?, locale, acceptPrivacy: true, website? (honeypot) }`; `url` without a scheme gets `https://` | `202 { id }`, the check runs in the background (the same email and host within 24 hours gives the earlier `id`); `400` field error `url` for an address that can't be checked; `429` past 5 a day per email — limited to 3/min/IP |
| GET | `/site-audits/:id` | `?locale=en` → `SiteAuditReport` `{ id, url, finalUrl, status, score, failure (why it couldn't be checked, FAILED only), categories: [{ key: speed \| mobile \| seo \| security, name, score, checks: [{ id, status: pass \| warn \| fail, title, advice }] }] (worst first), createdAt, finishedAt }` (texts in `locale`; no email or name); `404` when unknown. Limited to 60/min/IP |
| POST | `/newsletter` | `{ email, locale, acceptPrivacy: true, website? (honeypot) }` | `202` always, whether or not the address is subscribed (section 4.14). A new, `PENDING` or `UNSUBSCRIBED` address is emailed a link to `SITE_URL/{locale}/newsletter/confirm?token=…` (48 hours; at most one email per address every 10 minutes); an `ACTIVE` one is sent nothing; a filled honeypot does nothing. `400` field error `email: disposable` for a disposable address — limited to 5/min/IP |
| POST | `/newsletter/confirm` | `{ token }` | `200 { email, locale }`: the subscriber is `ACTIVE`. `400` when the token is unknown or expired — limited to 10/min/IP |
| POST | `/newsletter/unsubscribe` | `{ token }` (the subscriber's `unsubscribeToken`, from any newsletter email) | `200 { email }`, also when already unsubscribed; `404` for an unknown token — limited to 10/min/IP |
| POST | `/newsletter/unsubscribe/:token` | One-click unsubscribe (RFC 8058): body `List-Unsubscribe=One-Click`, urlencoded; it is the address in the emails' `List-Unsubscribe` header | `200`, same effect as above; `404` for an unknown token — limited to 60/min/IP |
| GET | `/newsletter/unsubscribe/:token` | For mail clients that open the `List-Unsubscribe` address in a browser | `302` to `SITE_URL/{locale}/newsletter/unsubscribe?token=…` (the subscriber's language; `en` for an unknown token); changes nothing — limited to 30/min/IP |
| GET | `/calculator` | The calculator's prices (`Setting` `calculator`) once published; `404` until then | |
| POST | `/drafts` | `Authorization: Bearer <release key>` + `{ title, body, version?, environment? (required with a version), url? }` | `201 UpdateDraft` (`{ id, project: { id, name }, title, body, version, environment, url, createdAt }`); the team inbox is emailed a link to review it. Same key rules and limit as `/releases` |
| POST | `/releases` | `Authorization: Bearer <release key>` + the body of `POST /admin/projects/:id/releases` | `201` release on the key's project, posted as "Despliegue automático" (shown as Codeqlick); `401` for a missing, unknown or revoked key — limited to 20/min/IP. For deploy pipelines: see below |
| GET | `/media/:filename` | | The uploaded image, cacheable forever (names are never reused); 404 otherwise |
| POST | `/auth/login` | `{ email, password, captcha? }` (`captcha`: the Turnstile token, required while `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` is set; `400` field error `captcha` when refused) | `200 AuthTokens`, or `200 { twoFactorRequired: true, challengeToken }` when two-step verification is on — limited to 5/min/IP |
| POST | `/auth/2fa/verify` | `{ challengeToken, code }` (authenticator or recovery code) | `200 AuthTokens`; `401` for a wrong code or an expired challenge — limited to 5/min/IP |
| POST | `/auth/refresh` | `{ refreshToken }` | `200 AuthTokens` |
| POST | `/auth/password-reset/request` | `{ email, locale }` | `202` always, whether or not the account exists. An active account is emailed a link to `SITE_URL/{locale}/reset-password?token=…`, or, when it was never registered, the invitation to create it — limited to 5/min/IP |
| POST | `/auth/register` | `{ name, email, password, locale, acceptPrivacy: true, website? (honeypot), captcha? }` (`captcha` as in login) | `202` always (it never says whether the email has an account); `400` field error `email: disposable` for a disposable address, `captcha` for a refused token. An email without a registered account is emailed a link to `SITE_URL/{locale}/register/confirm?token=…` (24 hours); one with a registered account is emailed that it already has one, with links to sign in and reset the password — limited to 3/min/IP |
| POST | `/auth/register/confirm` | `{ token }` | `200 AuthTokens`: the account is created (role CLIENT) or, when the team had prepared one for that email, it becomes theirs with the name and password they chose; signed in at once. `400` when the token is unknown, used or expired — limited to 5/min/IP |
| GET | `/auth/register/invitation?token=…` | | `{ email, name }` of the invitation (to fill in the form); `400` when invalid or expired |
| POST | `/auth/register/invitation` | `{ token, name, password, locale, acceptPrivacy: true }` | `200 AuthTokens`: the invitation's link proves the email, so the account is theirs at once — limited to 5/min/IP |
| POST | `/auth/password-reset/confirm` | `{ token, newPassword }` | `204`: password set, token spent, every session of the user revoked. `400` when the token is unknown, used or expired — limited to 5/min/IP |

### Authenticated (any role)

| Method | Path | Response |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/auth/logout` | `{ refreshToken }` → `204` |
| GET | `/auth/me` | `AuthUser` |
| GET / PATCH | `/auth/preferences` | `{ weeklyDigest, monthlyReport }` / either of them â the preferences |
| GET | `/auth/2fa` | `{ enabled, recoveryCodesLeft }` |
| POST | `/auth/2fa/setup` | `{ secret, otpauthUrl }`: a new secret, pending until confirmed; `409` when already on |
| POST | `/auth/2fa/enable` | `{ code }` → `{ recoveryCodes }` (shown once); `400` for a wrong code — limited to 5/min/IP |
| POST | `/auth/2fa/disable` | `{ password, code }` → `204`; `400` with a field error for a wrong password or code — limited to 5/min/IP |
| GET | `/auth/sessions` | `ActiveSession[]`: `{ id, userAgent, ip, lastActiveAt, current }`, one per signed-in device, most recent first (`id` is the family id) |
| DELETE | `/auth/sessions/:id` | `204`: signs that device out; `404` when it isn't the caller's or has already ended |
| POST | `/auth/sessions/revoke-others` | `{ refreshToken }` → `204`: signs out every device except the one owning `refreshToken` |
| POST | `/auth/password` | `{ currentPassword, newPassword, refreshToken }` → `204`. Revokes the caller's other sessions; the one owning `refreshToken` stays signed in. `400` with a field error when `currentPassword` is wrong or `newPassword` (min 12 chars) equals it — limited to 5/min/IP |
| PATCH | `/auth/me` | `{ name?, locale? }` → `AuthUser`: the caller's own name and email language |
| GET | `/auth/username/available` | `?username=` → `{ username, available, reason? }` (`reason`: `invalid` · `reserved` · `taken`; the caller's own name counts as available). 30/min |
| POST · DELETE | `/auth/me/avatar` | POST multipart `file` (PNG, JPEG or WebP up to 10 MB) → `AuthUser` with `avatar`: re-encoded to a 256×256 WebP square without metadata, replacing the old one; `400` not an image. DELETE → `AuthUser` without it. 10/min |
| GET | `/avatars/:file` | Public: a profile photo (`image/webp`, cached for a year: names are random and never reused) |
| PUT | `/auth/me/username` | `{ username }` (3–20 characters: `a-z`, `0-9`, `.` and `_`, starting and ending with a letter or digit, no `..`; upper case is lowered) → `AuthUser` with `username`; `400` invalid or reserved, `409` taken by someone else. `null` removes it. 10/min |
| POST | `/auth/email` | `{ email, password, locale }` → `202` (`400` field error `email: disposable` for a disposable address): emails a link to the new address (`SITE_URL/{locale}/confirm-email?token=…`, 24 hours; a new request replaces the pending one). `400` with a field error for a wrong password or the same email; `409` when the email already has an account — limited to 5/min/IP |
| POST | `/auth/email/confirm` | `{ token }` (no sign-in needed: the link may open on another device) → `{ email }`: the account's email changes, every session ends (they sign in again with the new address) and the old address is told. `400` when the token is unknown, used or expired; `409` when the email got an account meanwhile — limited to 5/min/IP |
| GET | `/auth/me/export` | Everything the platform keeps about the caller, as a JSON download (`codeqlick-<date>.json`; GDPR access and portability): account, sessions, projects they take part in, proposals, support requests and messages, comments on updates, managed-site edits, leads that became their account, meeting rooms, booking pages with their appointments, video chat profile, likes, blocks, reports made and sanctions. Never other people's data beyond names of the team and project titles. Limited to 3/hour |
| DELETE | `/auth/me` | `{ password }` → `204`: the account and everything that hangs from it is deleted (as an admin deleting it) and a goodbye email is sent. Refused: `403` for admin accounts (another admin deletes them); `409` for a client account that owns projects (contracts and invoices must be kept: the page offers a support request to close it). A person of a client company deletes just themself. `400` with a field error for a wrong password — limited to 5/min/IP |

| GET | `/videochat/me` | `{ onboarded, termsVersion, acceptedTermsVersion, suspended, suspension, ageVerificationRequired, ageVerified, allowed, profile, pushPublicKey, peakAlerts }` (section 4.7; `pushPublicKey` is the VAPID key, null while notifications are off; `peakAlerts` whether the account asked for peak time alerts); `suspension` is `{ reason, until, appeal: { status, response, createdAt } \| null }` or null, `profile` is `VideochatSettings` or null before onboarding |
| PUT | `/videochat/profile` | `VideochatSettings` `{ nickname, gender, seeking[], country, language, sameCountryOnly, sameLanguageOnly, interests[] }` (interests: up to 5 of the fixed list, section 4.7) → the same; `409` before onboarding |
| PUT | `/videochat/push` | `{ endpoint, keys: { p256dh, auth }, locale }` (a browser's push subscription) → `204`; `409` before onboarding or while notifications are off — limited to 10/min/IP |
| DELETE | `/videochat/push` | `{ endpoint }` → `204` (also when unknown) |
| PUT | `/videochat/push/peak` | `{ on }` → `{ on }`: peak time alerts for the account (section 4.7); `409` before onboarding |
| GET | `/videochat/stats` | `{ week: { chats, likes, contacts }, previousWeek }`: the person's last 7 days and the 7 before (section 4.7) |
| POST | `/videochat/verification` | multipart `file` → the status (`verification: { status: PENDING, note }`); `409` without a nickname, when verified already, before onboarding or while suspended; `400` not an image; `429` over 5 a day |
| GET | `/videochat/verification/file` | My photo while it waits for review |
| GET | `/videochat/contacts` | `{ items: [{ contactId, nickname, gender, country, language, interests, verified, online, busy, since }] }`: mutual contacts, newest first (section 4.7) |
| DELETE | `/videochat/contacts/:id` | → `204`: removes my like, so we are no longer contacts |
| POST | `/videochat/appeal` | `{ message }` → `204`; `409` when not suspended or the suspension was already appealed — limited to 5/min/IP |
| PUT | `/videochat/me` | `{ birthDate: 'YYYY-MM-DD', acceptTerms: true, termsVersion }` → the status; `403` under 18 (nothing stored), `409` for an old `termsVersion` — limited to 10/min/IP |
| GET | `/meetings/rooms` | `{ items: [{ id, code, title, lobby, createdAt, lastUsedAt, live }] }`: my meeting rooms, newest first (`live`: people inside now) |
| POST | `/meetings/rooms` | `{ title }` → the room; `409` past 20 rooms |
| PATCH | `/meetings/rooms/:id` | `{ title?, lobby? }` → the room (a live meeting follows); `404` when not mine |
| DELETE | `/meetings/rooms/:id` | `204`; a meeting going on in it ends |
| POST | `/meetings/rooms/:code/ticket` | Like the guest's ticket, with my account's name; the owner's ticket goes straight in |
| GET | `/citas/pages` | `{ items }`: my booking pages, with `upcoming` (confirmed appointments to come) and `meetingCode` |
| POST | `/citas/pages` | A page's fields → the page; `409` for a taken link or past 10 pages |
| PATCH | `/citas/pages/:id` | Any of the fields → the page; `404` when not mine |
| DELETE | `/citas/pages/:id` | `204`; its upcoming appointments are cancelled and their people emailed |
| GET | `/citas/appointments?scope=upcoming\|past` | `{ items }`: appointments on my pages, with the page's title |
| POST | `/citas/appointments/:id/cancel` | The owner cancels one; the visitor is emailed |
| GET | `/swipe/me` | Swipe status (section 4.10): `{ onboarded, termsVersion, acceptedTermsVersion, suspended, suspension: { reason, until } \| null, allowed, profile: { nickname, gender, seeking, interests } \| null, verification: { status, note, hasPhoto }, canUpload }` |
| PUT | `/swipe/me` | `{ birthDate, acceptTerms: true, termsVersion }` → the status; `403` under 18 (nothing stored), `409` for an old `termsVersion`. 10/min |
| PUT | `/swipe/profile` | `{ nickname, gender?, seeking, interests }` → the status |
| POST | `/swipe/verification` | Multipart `file` + `consent=true` → the status (verification PENDING); `400` not an image, `409` without a nickname. 10/min |
| GET | `/swipe/verification/file` | My verification photo (`image/webp`); `404` when none |
| GET | `/swipe/photos` | `{ items: [{ id, ref, status, rejectReason, likes, width, height, inAlbum, createdAt }] }`: my photos |
| POST | `/swipe/photos` | Multipart `file` + `consent=true` (+ `album=true` for the private album) → the photo (PENDING); `400` not an image or a removed one, `403` before the verification is approved, `409` past 10 public photos or 5 album photos, `429` past 10 uploads a day |
| PATCH | `/swipe/photos/:id` | `{ album }` → the photo, moved into or out of the private album (no new review); `409` when the destination is full |
| DELETE | `/swipe/photos/:id` | `204`; the file is deleted |
| GET | `/swipe/photos/:id/file` | The photo (`image/webp`, `Cache-Control: private, max-age=300`) for its owner, admins or a person who may see it in the deck (an album photo: only someone matched with its owner); otherwise `404` |
| GET | `/swipe/deck` | `{ items: [{ id, ref, width, height, nickname, gender, interests, bio, verified, lovesYou }] }`: up to 10 photos to vote on; `403` before onboarding or while suspended |
| POST | `/swipe/votes` | `{ photoId, like }` → `{ match: { id, nickname } \| null }` (a new match only once). 300/h |
| GET | `/swipe/matches` | `{ items: [{ id, partner: { nickname, gender, interests, bio, verified, photoId, albumPhotos }, lastMessage: { text, mine, at } \| null, unread, createdAt }] }` |
| GET | `/swipe/stats` | `{ week: { views, likes, loves, matches }, previousWeek }`: what happened to my photos in the last 7 days and the 7 before |
| GET | `/swipe/matches/:id/album` | `{ items: [{ id, ref, width, height }] }`: the partner's live album photos (empty when either blocked or they are suspended); `404` when the match isn't mine |
| GET | `/swipe/matches/:id/messages` | `?after=<ISO>` → `{ items: [{ id, mine, text, createdAt }] }` (the last 100, or newer than `after`); marks them read; `404` when not mine |
| POST | `/swipe/matches/:id/messages` | `{ text }` (1–1000) → the message. 20/min |
| DELETE | `/swipe/matches/:id` | `204`: ends the match and deletes its chat |
| POST | `/swipe/matches/:id/block` | `204`: ends the match and blocks the person both ways |
| POST | `/swipe/reports` | `{ photoId \| matchId, reason, details? }` → `204`; MINOR, NON_CONSENSUAL and STOLEN hide the photo at once. 20 a day |
| POST | `/videochat/ticket` | `{ ticket, expiresIn: 30, iceServers: { urls, username?, credential? }[] }`; `409` before onboarding, `403` suspended or age not verified when required, `429` past 10/min per account |

### Client portal (role `CLIENT`; admins may also call)

| Method | Path | Response |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/portal/projects` | `ClientProject[]` owned by the caller, each with its `milestones[]` |
| GET | `/portal/todo` | What waits on the client, oldest first: `{ items: [{ kind, id, title, projectId, projectName, since }] }`, `kind` being `approval` (an update asking for approval, `id` the update's), `proposal` (sent, not expired), `ticket` (a request `WAITING_CLIENT`, `title` with its `#number`), `brief` (questionnaire requested, not sent; `id` the project's) `review` (review requested; `id` the project's) or `invoice` (a team invoice not paid; `id` the invoice's, `title` its number, `since` its `issuedOn`) |
| GET | `/portal/projects/:id` | `ClientProject` with `updates[]` and `files[]` (`{ id, name, contentType, size, createdAt }`), newest first, and `milestones[]` (`{ id, title, description, dueDate, completedAt }`) in the admin's order; 404 if not the caller's |
| POST | `/portal/projects/:id/updates/:updateId/comments` | `{ body }` → `201 UpdateComment` (`{ id, body, createdAt, author: { name, team } }`). A client's comment emails the team inbox (`LEADS_NOTIFY_TO`, reply-to the client) |
| POST | `/portal/projects/:id/updates/:updateId/approval` | Role CLIENT only (403 for admins) → `ProjectUpdate` with `approvedAt`; 400 when the update doesn't ask for approval; approving again keeps the first approval. Emails the team inbox |
| GET / PUT | `/portal/projects/:id/brief` | `{ status: 'NONE' | 'REQUESTED' | 'SUBMITTED', answers, requestedAt, submittedAt, updatedAt }` / `{ answers, submit? }`: saves; with `submit`, sends it (400 listing `answers.<key>` when an essential one is missing) and emails the team inbox |
| GET / PUT | `/portal/projects/:id/review` | The review (`status: 'NONE'` when not asked for) / `{ rating, quote, authorName, authorRole?, consent, locale }` (400 when not asked for); emails the team inbox. Sending again puts a published one back to review |
| GET / POST | `/portal/projects/:id/credentials` | List (`{ id, label, url, username, createdBy: { name }, createdAt, viewedAt }`, never the secret) / `{ label, url?, username?, secret, notes? }` → `201` |
| DELETE | `/portal/projects/:id/credentials/:credentialId` | `204` |
| GET | `/portal/projects/:id/status` | `SiteMonitor` view (`{ status, statusSince, uptime30, days: [{ day, checks, ratio }] (30 Madrid days), certExpiresAt, lastCheckedAt, ... }`); 404 unless the project is the caller's and monitored |
| GET / PUT | `/portal/projects/:id/site` | The client's managed website: `{ status, preset, url, previewUrl, domain: { name, state, checkedAt, mail, nicHandle, serverIp }, lastBuildAt, businessName, colors: { primary, primaryText }, editable, unpublishedSince, legalMissing }` (`unpublishedSince`: when the editor's draft was saved, if it differs from the published site, else null; `legalMissing`: `taxId`/`address` the published legal data lacks; `nicHandle` and `serverIp` are what the client sets at their registrar), `editable` being what they may change (`tagline`, `contact` — with `reviewUrl`, the Google review link of section 8.2 —, `hours`, `hero: { title, text }`, `services`, `about: { title, paragraphs }`, `gallery`, `testimonials`; brand, theme and legal data stay with the team) / `{ site: editable }` → merged into the content, validated as a whole (400 with the field in the message), saved as a change by the client (a revision) and published; the team inbox is emailed what changed (or that publishing failed). Changing the hours drops the machine-readable `openingHoursSpec`. 404 unless the project is the caller's and has a managed site. Limited to 30/hour |
| PUT | `/portal/projects/:id/site/domain` | `{ name: string \| null }` → the site: the client chooses, changes or drops their own domain; it is rebuilt and the domain checked right away. 400 for a Codeqlick domain, 409 when another site uses it. Limited to 10/hour |
| GET / PUT / DELETE | `/portal/projects/:id/site/draft` | The website editor (section 8.2): `{ content, preset, presets: [{ key, theme, images }], published: { content, preset }, draft: { updatedAt, previewUrl, previewStatus } \| null, bookingPages: [{ slug, title, url, enabled }], menuEnabled, newsEnabled }` (`content`/`preset`: the draft's, or the published ones without a draft; `bookingPages`: the Citas pages of the people of the client account, for the site's booking button) / `{ content, preset?, rebuild? }` → the same: saved as the draft (400 with every issue as `{ path, message }`, nothing saved), its preview built when `rebuild` (or when there was none) / `204`: the draft is discarded and its preview deleted. Limited to 600/hour |
| POST | `/portal/projects/:id/site/draft/publish` | The draft becomes the site's content (a revision of the previous one) and is published; the team inbox is emailed what changed. 409 without a draft. Limited to 30/hour |
| POST | `/portal/projects/:id/site/draft/style` | `{ preset }` → the editor view: the draft takes that style's colours, fonts, corners, section order and photos (uploaded photos stay), keeping every text |
| GET | `/site-drafts/:key/*` | Public (the key is the secret): a file of the draft's preview, streamed from the builder; 404 when it doesn't exist or was discarded |
| POST | `/portal/projects/:id/site/images` | Multipart `file` (JPEG, PNG or WebP, 5 MB) → `201 { file: 'media/<name>', url }`, stored with the uploaded images (an image a managed site uses counts as used in the media library). Limited to 20/hour |
| GET | `/portal/projects/:id/visits` | `?days=7\|30\|90` (default 30) → `{ enabled, available, days, visitors, pageviews, messages, daily: [{ date, visitors, pageviews, messages }], referrers: [{ source, visitors }] (5) }`: the managed site's numbers from Umami and its form's messages; `enabled: false` until it has statistics. Also `GET /admin/projects/:id/visits`. 404 without a managed site of the caller's |
| GET | `/portal/projects/:id/site/messages` | `?page&pageSize` → `Paginated<{ id, name, email, phone, message, createdAt }>`: the messages the site's form kept (12 months), newest first. 404 without a managed site of the caller's |
| DELETE | `/portal/projects/:id/site/messages/:messageId` | `204`: the client deletes a message (for example when the visitor asks); 404 if it isn't one of that site's |
| GET / POST | `/portal/projects/:id/site/news` | News of the managed site (section 8.2): GET `?status=DRAFT\|PUBLISHED\|EXPIRED&page&pageSize` → `Paginated<SiteNewsPost>` (shape below), newest first, with `enabled` (the switch) beside it / `{ title, summary, body, photo?, expiresAt? (YYYY-MM-DD) }` → `201 SiteNewsPost` as a draft (400 with the field in the message, or past 200 posts; 400 when `photo` isn't a file of the media library, i.e. one uploaded through `/site/images`). Nothing is built. 404 unless the project is the caller's and has a managed site. Writes limited to 60/hour |
| GET / PATCH / DELETE | `/portal/projects/:id/site/news/:postId` | `SiteNewsPost` / the same fields, all optional (the slug never changes; `photo: null` and `expiresAt: null` remove them) → `SiteNewsPost`; the site is rebuilt when the post is published / `204`, the site rebuilt when the post was published. 404 if it isn't one of that site's |
| POST | `/portal/projects/:id/site/news/:postId/publish` · `/unpublish` | → `SiteNewsPost`: publishing sets `publishedAt` (kept on later edits) and rebuilds the site (409 when `expiresAt` is already past); unpublishing clears it and rebuilds. The answer carries the build's result as `build: 'OK' \| 'FAILED'`; 503 when the builder isn't configured, with the post unchanged. Limited to 30/hour |
| PUT | `/portal/projects/:id/site/news-settings` | `{ enabled }` → `{ enabled }`: switches the section on or off; the site is rebuilt. Limited to 30/hour |
| GET | `/portal/projects/:id/tasks` | The project's tasks without `description` (`{ id, title, status, ticket, doneAt, createdAt, updatedAt }`) |
| GET | `/portal/projects/:id/plan` | `{ active, name, monthlyMinutes, alertPercent, month, usedMinutes, remainingMinutes, percent, months: [{ month, minutes }] (4, newest first) }`; 404 unless the project is the caller's and the plan is active |
| GET | `/portal/projects/:id/time` | `ProjectTime` (`{ totalMinutes, months: [{ month: 'yyyy-mm', minutes }], entries: TimeEntry[] }`, newest first); 404 unless the project is the caller's and has `showHours` |
| GET | `/portal/projects/:id/files/:fileId` | The file, always as `application/octet-stream` with `Content-Disposition: attachment` (UTF-8 name); 404 if the project isn't the caller's |
| GET | `/portal/invoices` | Every invoice of the caller's account, newest first: `{ items: Invoice[] }` (shape below), the team's (`source: 'team'`) and those Stripe issued for websites bought online (`source: 'stripe'`, decision 20) |
| GET | `/portal/invoices/:id/file` | A team invoice's PDF, as an `application/pdf` attachment; 404 if it isn't the caller's |
| GET | `/portal/invoices/stripe/:purchaseId` | `{ url }`: the PDF of the invoice Stripe issued for that purchase, asked to Stripe at that moment (its links don't last); 404 if the purchase isn't of one of the caller's projects or has no invoice |
| GET | `/portal/proposals` | `?status&page&pageSize` → `Paginated<Proposal>` of the caller's proposals, drafts left out |
| GET | `/portal/proposals/:id` | `Proposal`; 404 for a draft or someone else's |
| POST | `/portal/proposals/:id/accept` | Role CLIENT only (403 for admins). `{ name }` → `Proposal` `ACCEPTED`; 409 unless `SENT` and not expired. Emails the team inbox |
| POST | `/portal/proposals/:id/decline` | Role CLIENT only. `{ reason? }` → `Proposal` `DECLINED`; 409 unless `SENT`. Emails the team inbox |
| GET / POST | `/portal/tickets` | GET `?page&pageSize` → `Paginated<Ticket>` of the caller's requests, latest activity first. POST (role CLIENT only) `{ type, priority?, title, body, projectId? }` → `201 TicketDetail`; 400 when `projectId` isn't one of the caller's projects. Emails the team inbox (`LEADS_NOTIFY_TO`, reply-to the client) |
| GET | `/portal/tickets/:id` | `TicketDetail`; 404 if it isn't the caller's |
| POST | `/portal/tickets/:id/messages` | `{ body }` → `TicketDetail`. A request `WAITING_CLIENT` or `RESOLVED` goes back to `IN_PROGRESS`; 409 when it is `CLOSED`. Emails the team inbox |
| POST | `/portal/tickets/:id/close` | → `TicketDetail` with status `CLOSED` (no more replies) |
| POST | `/portal/tickets/:id/files` | Multipart, one `file` field, up to 25 MB; the request's client or an admin → `201 TicketDetail`; 409 when `CLOSED`. A client's file emails the team inbox |
| GET | `/portal/tickets/:id/files/:fileId` | The file, always as `application/octet-stream` attachment; 404 if the request isn't the caller's (admins may read any) |

### Cloud (section 4.13; a session or, where marked 🔑, an API token)

Team routes take the team's `slug`; project routes the project's `id`. Not a member: `404`. A role too low: `403`. A token restricted to another team: `404`. Every change is a `CloudEvent`.

| Method | Path | Body / query | Response |
|---|---|---|---|
| POST | `/cloud/cli/device` | Public. `{ clientName }` (1–60, the machine's name) | `{ deviceCode, userCode, verificationUri, verificationUriComplete, expiresIn: 600, interval: 5 }`. 10/min/IP |
| POST | `/cloud/cli/device/token` | Public. `{ deviceCode }` | `{ status: 'pending' \| 'denied' \| 'expired' }` or, once, `{ status: 'approved', token, user: { id, name, email } }`; polling faster than every 5 s answers `429` |
| GET | `/cloud/cli/device/:userCode` | | `{ userCode, clientName, createdAt, expiresAt, status }`; `404` unknown or expired |
| POST | `/cloud/cli/device/:userCode` | `{ approve: boolean }` | `204` |
| GET | `/cloud/me` 🔑 | | `{ user: { id, name, email }, via: 'session' \| 'token', token: { id, name, teamId } \| null, teams: [{ id, slug, name, role, plan }] }` |
| GET | `/cloud/tokens` | | `{ items: [{ id, name, prefix, team: { slug, name } \| null, expiresAt, lastUsedAt, lastUsedIp, createdAt }] }` (mine) |
| POST | `/cloud/tokens` | `{ name, teamSlug?, expiresInDays: 30 \| 90 \| 365 \| null }` | `201 { token, item }` (`token` shown once); `409` past 20 |
| DELETE | `/cloud/tokens/:id` | | `204` |
| POST | `/cloud/teams` | `{ name, slug, acceptTerms: true, termsVersion }` | `201` the team; `409` slug taken or past 3 owned / 10 joined |
| GET / PATCH / DELETE | `/cloud/teams/:team` | PATCH `{ name }` (ADMIN) | `{ id, slug, name, plan, role, createdAt }` / the team / `204` (OWNER; its projects deleted) |
| GET | `/cloud/teams/:team/members` | | `{ items: [{ userId, name, email, role, joinedAt }], invites: [{ id, email, role, expiresAt }] }` |
| POST | `/cloud/teams/:team/invites` | ADMIN. `{ email, role: 'ADMIN' \| 'MEMBER', locale }` | `201` the invite (emailed); `409` already a member, invited, or 10 members |
| DELETE | `/cloud/teams/:team/invites/:id` | ADMIN | `204` |
| GET | `/cloud/invites/:token` | | `{ team: { name, slug }, role, invitedBy, email, expiresAt, emailMatches }`; `404` unknown, expired or used |
| POST | `/cloud/invites/:token/accept` | `{ acceptTerms: true, termsVersion }` | `{ team }`; `403` when my email isn't the invited one |
| PATCH | `/cloud/teams/:team/members/:userId` | `{ role }` (OWNER for owners; ADMIN otherwise) | `204`; `409` would leave no owner |
| DELETE | `/cloud/teams/:team/members/:userId` | ADMIN, or myself to leave | `204`; `409` the last owner |
| GET | `/cloud/teams/:team/usage` 🔑 | | `{ plan, limits, projects, storageBytes, deploymentsLastHour, members, meters }`; `meters` (usage alerts) is `[{ key: 'builds' \| 'formSubmissions' \| 'analyticsEvents' \| 'storage' \| 'deploymentsPerHour', used, limit, percent }]`, `percent` being `floor(used / limit × 100)` (may pass 100); the console shows its banner from it |
| GET | `/cloud/teams/:team/events` 🔑 | `?page&pageSize` | `Paginated<CloudEvent>` newest first |
| GET | `/cloud/teams/:team/projects` 🔑 | | `{ items: CloudProject[] }` |
| POST | `/cloud/teams/:team/projects` 🔑 | ADMIN. `{ name, slug }` | `201 CloudProject`; `409` slug taken or reserved, or past 10 |
| GET / PATCH / DELETE | `/cloud/projects/:id` 🔑 | PATCH `{ name }` (ADMIN) | `CloudProject` / `CloudProject` / `204` (ADMIN, see 4.13) |
| GET | `/cloud/projects/:id/deployments` 🔑 | `?page&pageSize` | `Paginated<CloudDeployment>` newest first |
| POST | `/cloud/projects/:id/deployments` 🔑 | Multipart: `bundle` (tar.gz), `target` (`preview` \| `production`), `message?` (≤ 200), `commit?` (hex, ≤ 40), `branch?` (≤ 100), `config?` (the text of `codeqlick.json`, 4.13) | `201 CloudDeployment`; `400` with the reason (an invalid `config` names the path and lists `errors: [{ path, message }]`), `403` suspended, `413` too big, `429` past 30/h |
| GET / DELETE | `/cloud/projects/:id/deployments/:deploymentId` 🔑 | | `CloudDeployment` / `204` (`409` when production) |
| POST | `/cloud/projects/:id/deployments/:deploymentId/promote` 🔑 | | `CloudProject` with it as production; `403` suspended |
| GET | `/cloud/projects/:id/domains` 🔑 | | `{ items: CloudDomain[] }` |
| POST | `/cloud/projects/:id/domains` 🔑 | `{ name }` | `201 CloudDomain` (with what to add to the DNS); `400` not allowed, `409` taken or past 5 |
| POST | `/cloud/projects/:id/domains/:domainId/check` 🔑 | | `CloudDomain` checked now. 10/min |
| DELETE | `/cloud/projects/:id/domains/:domainId` 🔑 | | `204` |
| GET / PUT | `/cloud/projects/:id/protection` 🔑 | PUT `{ mode: 'OFF' \| 'PREVIEWS' \| 'ALL', password? (8–128), days?: 1 \| 7 \| 30 }` (ADMIN) | `CloudProtection`; `400` invalid or turning it on without a password, `503` without `CLOUD_PROTECTION_KEY` (turning it on) |
| GET | `/cloud/projects/:id/protection/ticket` 🔑 | `?host` (one of the project's addresses) | `{ url, expiresIn: 60 }`: `https://<host>/__codeqlick/enter?ticket=…`, or the plain `https://<host>/` when that address isn't protected; `400` not one of the project's addresses |
| GET | `/cloud/schema/codeqlick.json` | Public, cached a day | The JSON Schema of `codeqlick.json` (`application/schema+json`, CORS for any origin) |
| POST | `/cloud/config/check` 🔑 | Multipart (a small JSON body works too) with fields `teamSlug` and `config` (the file's text, as in the deployment upload; any member) | `{ ok: true, summary: { redirects, rewrites, headers, cleanUrls, trailingSlash } }` or `{ ok: false, errors: [{ path, message }] }` (both `200`; limits of the team's plan; nothing is kept). `400` when `config` isn't a string or is over 1 MB (the multipart field takes up to 2 MB, so the answer is a `400`, not a `413`) |
| GET | `/cloud/edge/check` | Nginx only (`X-Cq-Protection-Key`), one call per request that needs it (password protection and `codeqlick.json` rules, 4.13). Headers `X-Cq-Host`, `X-Cq-Project`, `X-Cq-Kind` (`previews` \| `production`), `X-Cq-Access`, `X-Cq-Deployment` (id), `X-Cq-Uri` (the request's decoded path), `X-Cq-Args` (its query string) | `204` serve, with `X-Cq-Path` (the file path to serve; `/.cq-none` when no file answers; empty when the deployment has no rules) and `X-Cq-H-<name>` for each header to add; `401` ask for the password; `403` redirect, with `X-Cq-Status` (`301` \| `302` \| `307` \| `308`) and `X-Cq-Location`; `403` without `X-Cq-Status` = wrong key. Never reachable from api.codeqlick.com |
| GET | `/cloud/protection/check` | Deprecated alias of `GET /cloud/edge/check` (same handler and answers), kept for one release so Nginx snippets that predate it keep working; removed afterwards | as `/cloud/edge/check` |
| GET / POST | `/cloud/protection/password` | Nginx only, as `/__codeqlick/password` on the hosting address. GET `?next`; POST form `password`, `next` | The password page (HTML, EN/ES); POST right: `303` to `next` with the cookie, wrong: the page with the error (`401`), `429` past 5/min per IP and project |
| GET | `/cloud/protection/enter` | Nginx only, as `/__codeqlick/enter`. `?ticket` | `303` to `/` with the cookie; the password page when the ticket is invalid |
| GET / POST | `/cloud/projects/:id/forms` 🔑 | POST `{ name, notify, recipientIds }` (ADMIN) | `{ items: CloudForm[] }` / `201 CloudForm`; `409` past the plan's forms |
| PATCH / DELETE | `/cloud/projects/:id/forms/:formId` 🔑 | PATCH `{ name?, enabled?, notify?, recipientIds? }` (ADMIN) | `CloudForm` / `204` (its submissions too) |
| POST | `/cloud/projects/:id/forms/:formId/key` 🔑 | ADMIN | `CloudForm` with a new key |
| GET | `/cloud/projects/:id/forms/:formId/submissions` 🔑 | `?q&page&pageSize` | `Paginated<{ id, data, email, page, createdAt }>` newest first |
| GET | `/cloud/projects/:id/forms/:formId/submissions.csv` 🔑 | | `text/csv` |
| DELETE | `/cloud/projects/:id/forms/:formId/submissions/:submissionId` 🔑 | | `204` |
| POST | `/cloud/forms/:key` | Public. The form's fields (see Forms) | JSON `{ ok }`, a `303` back to the site, or a short page; `400` invalid or with files, `403` other origin, `404` unknown or off, `429` |
| GET / POST | `/cloud/teams/:team/monitors` 🔑 | POST `{ name, kind, url? \| periodMinutes?, graceMinutes?, projectId?, alertIds }` (ADMIN) | `{ items: CloudMonitor[] }` / `201 CloudMonitor` (heartbeats with their URL); `400` an unsafe URL, `409` past 10 |
| GET / PATCH / DELETE | `/cloud/monitors/:id` 🔑 | PATCH `{ name?, enabled?, url?, periodMinutes?, graceMinutes?, alertIds? }` (ADMIN) | `CloudMonitor` with `checks` (last 24 h), `days` (90) and `events` (30 days) / `CloudMonitor` / `204` |
| GET / POST | `/cloud/heartbeats/:key` | Public; `?status=fail` or `{ "status": "fail" }` | `200 { ok: true }`; `404` unknown or paused. 30/min per key |
| GET / PUT | `/cloud/teams/:team/status-page` 🔑 | PUT `{ enabled, title, intro?, items: [{ monitorId, label }] }` (ADMIN) | The page's settings |
| GET | `/cloud/status/:team` | Public, cached 60 s | `{ title, intro, overall, monitors: [{ label, status, uptime90, days, incidents }] }`; `404` without an enabled page |
| GET | `/cloud/status/:team/badge.svg` | Public, cached 60 s | `image/svg+xml` |
| POST | `/cloud/audits` 🔑 | `{ url, teamSlug, locale? }` | The report (see Website check API); `422` couldn't open it, `429` past 20/h, `504` over 60 s |
| GET | `/cloud/teams/:team/audits` 🔑 | `?page&pageSize` | `Paginated<{ id, url, score, preview, createdAt }>` (`preview`: made for a build, decision 52) |
| GET | `/cloud/audits/:id` 🔑 | `?locale` | The report |
| GET | `/cloud/github/install-url` | `?teamSlug` (ADMIN) | `{ url }`: the app's installation page with a signed state; `503` when the app isn't set up |
| POST | `/cloud/github/installations` | `{ installationId, code, state }` | `{ installation: { id, accountLogin } }` after checking with the person's GitHub token; `403` when they can't see it, `409` linked to another team |
| GET | `/cloud/teams/:team/github` 🔑 | | `{ configured, installations: [{ id, accountLogin, accountType }] }` |
| GET | `/cloud/teams/:team/github/repos` 🔑 | `?installationId` | `{ items: [{ id, fullName, defaultBranch, private }] }` (the installation's repositories) |
| PUT / DELETE | `/cloud/projects/:id/git` 🔑 | PUT `{ installationId, repoId, branch, rootDir?, installCommand?, buildCommand?, outputDir?, nodeVersion?, buildBranches?, buildPullRequests?, previewChecks? }` (ADMIN) | `CloudProject` with its `git`; DELETE disconnects |
| GET / PUT / DELETE | `/cloud/projects/:id/env` 🔑 | PUT `{ key, value, production, preview }` (ADMIN); DELETE `?key` | `{ items: [{ key, production, preview, updatedAt }] }` (never values) |
| GET / POST | `/cloud/projects/:id/builds` 🔑 | GET `?page`; POST `{ branch?, noCache? }` (a build of the branch's latest commit; `noCache: true` runs it without the dependency cache and is stored on the build) | `Paginated<CloudBuild>` (each with `audit`) / `201 CloudBuild`; `409` without a repository, `429` past the month's builds |
| GET | `/cloud/projects/:id/builds/:buildId` 🔑 | `?after` (log offset) | The build (with `audit`) and its log (from `after`) |
| POST | `/cloud/projects/:id/builds/:buildId/cancel` 🔑 | | The build (`CANCELED`; a running one is stopped by the agent at its next heartbeat) |
